## INTRODUCTION

During the 1950s, the first Artificial intelligence machine was proposed by Alan Turing \[[1](#9789815305128-C2.html#r1)\], which marked the beginning of machine learning applications. Machine learning was first used for face detection for security services \[[2](#9789815305128-C2.html#r2)\], and it has since been utilised in public transit to increase efficiency and lower danger \[[3](#9789815305128-C2.html#r3), [4](#9789815305128-C2.html#r4)\] as well as more recently in a variety of biotechnology and healthcare applications \[[5](#9789815305128-C2.html#r5)\]. Similar developments are predicted for medicine and healthcare. Machine learning and artificial intelligence have substantially altered daily life and corporate processes. This field has made remarkable strides recently, offering doctors relief from workloads and increasing precision, prognosis, and care quality.

The potential of a doctor or an analyst is to perform their own duties, recognize trends in healthcare, and create models for disease prediction which are mostly aided by recent advances in machine learning. Machine learning-based techniques have additionally been put into practice in large medical organizations in order to attain higher efficiency in the following areas: blood samples \[[5](#9789815305128-C2.html#r5)\], identification of irregularities in organs \[[6](#9789815305128-C2.html#r6)^(\-)[8](#9789815305128-C2.html#r8)\], robot-assisted surgeries \[[9](#9789815305128-C2.html#r9), [10](#9789815305128-C2.html#r10)\], and bones \[[11](#9789815305128-C2.html#r11)\], as well as the organization of health records electronically. Recently in the fight against COVID-19, hospital response times have been accelerated by the use of machine learning algorithms.

With GE's Clinical Command Centre, a deep learning technology, hospitals were capable of exchanging, tracking, and organizing patients, rooms, beds, EHRs, ventilators, as well as staff throughout the pandemonium \[[12](#9789815305128-C2.html#r12)\]. Artificial intelligence has also been utilized by researchers to monitor and identify SARS-CoV2 genetic sequences and to create vaccinations \[[13](#9789815305128-C2.html#r13)\].

As the medical sector expands into the modern technological era, numerous innovations appear. Advancements in the discipline, such as faster diagnostic times, greater accuracy, and ease of use, depend heavily on machine learning and artificial intelligence models and applications. Outlining the drawbacks and benefits of methods based on machine learning in the healthcare sector is the aim of this chapter.

We hope to give a concise summary of the many machine learning approaches and draw attention to the regions where these types of methods are mainly used since the healthcare business is engulfed in the use of new machine learning technologies. We talk about their extensive potential and application for the development of the future of the healthcare sector. We discuss the risks and challenges that arise from their application in terms of ethics and logistics.

## OVERVIEW OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE

While artificial intelligence, deep learning, and machine learning are sometimes used synonymously, they refer to distinct sets of algorithms and learning processes. The general name for any computerised intelligence that mimics and learns from human intelligence is Artificial Intelligence (AI) \[[14](#9789815305128-C2.html#r14)\]. While self-driving cars and robots are the most well-known examples of autonomous technology, AI is also used in many commonplace applications, including web searches and personalised marketing. Due to Artificial Intelligence's increased levels of decision-making, precision, problem-solving capacity, and computing skills, AI application and development have made great leaps in recent years and have been applied to many domains \[[15](#9789815305128-C2.html#r15)\].

In the process of developing Artificial Intelligence algorithms, the collected data is divided into two sets—a training set and a test set—in order to guarantee impartial forecasts, populations representative, and trustworthy learning. The training set, as its name implies, is used to train algorithms. It consists of sets of defining characteristics (data points) and, in the type of supervised learning, correlating predictions. The testing data set, which is basically new to the algorithm, is only used to evaluate its performance. This step is used to ensure that biases in the training dataset's algorithm testing are eliminated \[[16](#9789815305128-C2.html#r16)\]. When an algorithm produces satisfactory results during training and testing, it is put into use in healthcare environments.

The use of artificial intelligence (AI) is widespread and has numerous applications in various sub-regions; here, we give a summary of two of these sub-regions: deep learning and machine learning.

Machine learning is the application of various algorithms and statistical methods to address issues without the necessity for specialised programming \[[17](#9789815305128-C2.html#r17)\]. Since many machine learning models have only one layer, a significant part of data processing feature extraction must be finished before the data is entered into the algorithm \[[18](#9789815305128-C2.html#r18)\]. These types of machine learning techniques, in the absence of the additional layers, necessitate extensive data preparation to identify accurate predictions and prevent the training dataset from being over- or under-fitted.

Deep learning is treated as a more complex subset of machine learning in which users make use of layered artificial neural networks. It offers more precision and interpretability at the expense of less interpretability \[[19](#9789815305128-C2.html#r19)\]. According to its description, the neural network approach uses a multilayer network to support the connections between the units or artificial neurons, in each of the layers and the layers above and below \[[20](#9789815305128-C2.html#r20)\]. These multilevel links let these networks analyse data autonomously, allowing them to learn, perceive, and draw conclusions from it. The data are processed until specific outcomes are obtained.

### Different Learning Methodologies

The majority of AI and machine learning algorithms are based on several learning methodologies. Supervised learning is one subtype that is used to train algorithms for prediction and classification using prior instances or outputs. The fact that the training set for this learning method includes features and matching predictions, or outcomes, is a key differentiator. In short, supervised learning method uses details derived from the features of training data set to building an outcome-predicting model accurately from the training data set. The learned model is then used to utilize the new testing data set attributes to create predictions.

Some machine learning algorithms that use supervised learning approaches are Decision Trees, Support Vector Machines, Random Forests, and Artificial Neural Networks. Using a single node as the starting point, decision tree algorithms create a decision assistance tool that shows the potential outcomes of it. After making that decision and subsequent ones, the tree continues until it reaches the desired outcome \[[21](#9789815305128-C2.html#r21)\]. Classification techniques called Support Vector Machines (SVM) identify the biggest margin hyperplane for dividing data and provide the best fit for arranging it. SVMs employ supervised learning for classifying features in two group issues \[[22](#9789815305128-C2.html#r22)\]. Three layers make up artificial neural networks, or ANNs: input, output, and one or more hidden layers. Each layer's neurons are linked to every other layer's neurons both ahead and behind it \[[23](#9789815305128-C2.html#r23)\]. Supervised machine learning techniques are extensively used in the healthcare industry for a variety of purposes, including image detection, hospital outcome identification, and disease prediction.

Unsupervised learning, which is commonly employed for data evaluation and application clustering, is another subset of AI-based learning techniques. When it comes to data analysis, stratification, and reduction, unsupervised machine learning is typically used for goals other than prediction. In general, algorithms are used in unsupervised clustering techniques to organize data into separate groups that have not yet been identified. Despite the fact that most machine learning approaches involve feature extraction and preprocessing of the data before the input, this approach enables feature extraction and investigates potential data clusters by finding the underlying features or relationships of the data and then classifying them according to their similarities.

Deep Belief Networks (DBN), CNN (Convolutional neural networks), and the k-means method are a few unsupervised learning techniques. k-Means algorithm is the most popular unsupervised learning algorithm, which is employed as a clustering technique to find the mean across groups in unlabeled type of datasets and form groups according to the mean. Deep belief network (DBN) is a multilayer network with several hidden layers that are responsible for the feature recognition and correlation analysis in the data. It is composed of intra-level connections that are helpful for the data retrieval and is usually based on unsupervised learning \[[24](#9789815305128-C2.html#r24), [25](#9789815305128-C2.html#r25)\]. Since convolutional neural networks (CNNs) rely on feature recognition and identification, they are multilayer networks that function well for image recognition, anomaly detection, and identification.

Because the data is homogeneous and there are no predetermined results, several unsupervised algorithms are utilised for clustering; nonetheless, despite their usefulness and speed, unsupervised techniques are only marginally popular in the healthcare industry.

Another technique for learning that falls between supervised and unsupervised learning is reinforcement learning. This learning is reliant on reward sequences, much like the psychological mechanisms of conditioning, and it develops a strategy for functioning within a particular problem domain. Reinforcement learning approaches have been called the closest to animal and human learning since they optimise the error criteria and have the ability to affect their surroundings \[[26](#9789815305128-C2.html#r26)\].

The choice of learning methods is typically determined by the implementation goal and is comparatively less complex than the choice of algorithms given the variety of learning approaches. The Recurrent Neural Network (RNN) is a popular neural network that makes use of reinforcement learning. An RNN is a type of neural network in which all artificial neurons are connected to one another. This allows the artificial neurons to reuse the outputs of one phase as input for another and to receive inputs with time delays. Translation, time series prediction, rhythm acquisition, speech recognition, as well as creation of music are among its many applications.

Even while the use of reinforcement learning in healthcare is still restricted because of its requirements for structure, diverse data, the formulation and application of the rewards, and large computational resources, still it has the potential to significantly advance in the field.

A viable solution that is relevant to the healthcare application must be identified and implemented, given the variety of deep learning and machine learning approaches available. The amount of sample size \[[27](#9789815305128-C2.html#r27), [28](#9789815305128-C2.html#r28)\], features, and data distributions \[[29](#9789815305128-C2.html#r29)\] are a few variables that need to be taken into account as they have a significant effect on the processes of learning and forecasting.

### Healthcare Applications of Artificial Intelligence

The sector of healthcare has been seeing advances in machine learning for years. AI applications have the potential to help with a variety of tasks, including case triage as well as diagnosis \[[30](#9789815305128-C2.html#r30)\], improved image segmentation and scanning \[[31](#9789815305128-C2.html#r31)\], decision-making support, neuroimaging, and illness risk prediction. Here, we provide a quick rundown of the latest developments in Artificial Intelligence applications to particular areas of health care. The greater availability of the digital data utilised in ML-based techniques and the apps' clinical applications and experiments in addition to learning methodologies serve as the basis for inclusion criteria for the applications listed.

In the current study, we concentrated on the application of Machine Learning in the domains of medical imaging, genetic engineering, and electronic healthcare records. These domains also embody the BIG data in healthcare, namely the organised and disorganised data within the field, and have demonstrated noteworthy potential concerning clinical implementations.

The following is the search approach we will use: articles published between the months of June 2020 and December 2020; journal databases and online libraries, such as Academic, OneFile, Nature, Gale, Science Direct, Pubmed, and PsycNet. The papers and articles in the compilation concentrated on the present and future applications of Artificial Intelligence and machine learning for the healthcare industry. The search terms that came up were artificial intelligence for healthcare, medical imaging with machine learning, machine learning and big data, electronic health records, genomics with machine learning, medical applications of AI, and problems with AI in healthcare.

### Digital Health Records

In the 1960s, Lockheed introduced the first electronic health record (EHR), which was first called a clinical information system \[[32](#9789815305128-C2.html#r32)\]. The systems had undergone numerous reconstructions since then in an effort to establish a uniform system for the industry. To increase the quality and productivity of the work, the US federal government invested billions of dollars in 2009 to encourage the implementation of EHRs in all practices. As a result, by 2015, nationwide almost 87 percent office-based applications had integrated EHRs into their systems \[[33](#9789815305128-C2.html#r33)\]. Deep learning applications, such as prescription refills and the use of patient history to forecast diagnoses, have benefited greatly from BIG data gathered by EHR systems with feature data that is structured.

Physician diagnosis and treatment have been aided by this, which has led to notable advancements in the organization of data, accessibility of data, and quality. Researchers’ access to health records has been facilitated by the standardisation of features across databases.

Scientists created models using deep learning for diagnosis as well as prediction of clinical disorders using EHRs because they recognize the critical role played in therapy delivery using prediction. In a recent research, Zhang, Liu, and Razavian created an algorithm using deep learning to predict the occurrence of diseases like a failure of heart, stroke, and renal failure, utilizing CNNs (supervised learning) and LSTM networks (reinforcement learning).

This method employed both structured data by the EHR and unstructured data by the progress and diagnostic notes, in contrast to prior prediction models. Liu *et al.* (40,00) expounded that adding unstructured data into the model led to noteworthy enhancements in all the baseline accuracy metrics, hence underscoring the resilience and adaptability of these types of algorithms. Ge and his colleagues developed a model for predicting post-stroke pneumonia between 7 to 14 days in another study that used deep neural network approaches.

With an accuracy of 92.8 percent for 7^(th) day prediction and 90.5 percent for 14^(th) day predictions, the model yielded a highly accurate model for forecasting pneumonia after a stroke happened (AUC, model performance measure by combining the specificity and sensitivity of a model) \[[34](#9789815305128-C2.html#r34)\]. In order to forecast mortality in intensive care unit patients, a number of machine learning (ML) models have also been used. Ahmad and associates have demonstrated a high degree of predictive capacity in one of these models for predicting death rates among patients with paralytic ileus (PI, an incomplete intestinal blockage that impedes food transit and ultimately results in a buildup and total intestinal blockage) using electronic health records.

When predicting mortality for patients with peripheral artery disease, the algorithm known as the SRML - Statistically Robust Machine Learning based Predictor for Mortality has an accuracy rate of 81.30% \[[35](#9789815305128-C2.html#r35)\]. By utilising EHR prediction algorithms, healthcare providers and patients can make more informed decisions about clinical treatment when they are armed with expected mortality.

### Radiology

Medical imaging or radiology has made remarkable progress using machine learning techniques to several imaging modalities, which include magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), computed tomography (CT), positron emission tomography (PET), X-ray, ultrasound, and many more. This is due to the fact that data is digital and that structured data formats are available as DICOM (Digital Imaging and Communications in Medicine). To identify lesions \[[36](#9789815305128-C2.html#r36)\], tumors \[[37](#9789815305128-C2.html#r37), [38](#9789815305128-C2.html#r38)\], fractures \[[39](#9789815305128-C2.html#r39), [40](#9789815305128-C2.html#r40)\] and rips \[[41](#9789815305128-C2.html#r41), [42](#9789815305128-C2.html#r42)\], several machine learning models have been constructed.

McKinney and associates have employed an algorithm using deep learning in a new study to identify tumours at an earlier stage of growth based on mammograms. These deep learning-based screen techniques enable a higher rate of tumour resection by identifying and localising tumours at an earlier stage of breast cancer than would be possible with traditional screening methods. An AUC score of 11.5% allowed the method based on deep learning to beat seasoned radiologists with direct comparison \[[43](#9789815305128-C2.html#r43)\]. With differing degrees of effectiveness, a number of additional studies have also used Machine Learning techniques for the identification of breast cancer. These studies include models by Ahmad and others \[[44](#9789815305128-C2.html#r44)\], Wang and others \[[45](#9789815305128-C2.html#r45)\], and Amrane and others \[[46](#9789815305128-C2.html#r46)\].

Similar to this, Esteva and associates employed CNN (unsupervised learning) in recent work to categorize 2032 distinct skin conditions using dermoscopic pictures. The accuracy of the data was further supported by an impartial comparison by CNN's categorization with dermatologists certified by 21 board, which revealed performance “on par”. This method can lead to early diagnosis and ease of use when combined with the typical consumer mobile platform. Simultaneously, research has employed machine learning (ML) techniques to measure the advancement of retinal disorders \[[46](#9789815305128-C2.html#r46)^(\-)[48](#9789815305128-C2.html#r48)\]. Arcadu *et al.* conducted a study wherein they utilised a CNN using deep learning to identify the aneurysms responsible for visual loss resulting from the advancement of DR (Diabetic Retinopathy) \[[49](#9789815305128-C2.html#r49)\]. Despite not being specifically designed to do so, the CNN was also capable of identifying tiny, low-contraction microaneurysms \[[49](#9789815305128-C2.html#r49), [50](#9789815305128-C2.html#r50)\]. Diabetic retinopathy called a prevalent eye disorder affects 60% of people with type-1 diabetes \[[51](#9789815305128-C2.html#r51)\]. Consequently, it can be challenging to identify in its early stages. Timely prediction made possible by the CNN technique may help patients avoid or postpone permanent visual loss. For decades, X-rays have been used to detect lung diseases and anomalies in the chest cavity; however, a thorough, meticulous examination by a qualified radiologist is frequently necessary. In an effort to replicate the detection made by skilled radiologists, Rajpurkar and colleagues recently carried out a retrospective description for investigating the abilities of a convolutional neural network with 121-layer to analyse a set of chest x-rays with kinds of thoracic diseases and for identifying abnormalities.

Comparatively, CNN performed 2% better than radiologists in terms of identification accuracy of 81%. This study, like CNNs developed by Asif and others \[[52](#9789815305128-C2.html#r52)\], Tsai and Tao \[[53](#9789815305128-C2.html#r53)\], Lee and colleagues \[[54](#9789815305128-C2.html#r54)\], and Liang and colleagues \[[55](#9789815305128-C2.html#r55)\], shows the tremendous support that these methods may provide in diagnosing and examining illnesses, further reduced the burden on healthcare professionals, even though they are applied retrospectively.

In addition, ML-based methods have been used to diagnose and predict the course of serious mental illnesses such as depression \[[56](#9789815305128-C2.html#r56)\], psychosis \[[57](#9789815305128-C2.html#r57), [58](#9789815305128-C2.html#r58)\], PTSD \[[59](#9789815305128-C2.html#r59)\], Parkinson's disease \[[60](#9789815305128-C2.html#r60), [61](#9789815305128-C2.html#r61)\], Alzheimer's disease \[[62](#9789815305128-C2.html#r62)\], and various developmental disorders such as ADHD \[[63](#9789815305128-C2.html#r63), [64](#9789815305128-C2.html#r64)\] and autism \[[65](#9789815305128-C2.html#r65), [66](#9789815305128-C2.html#r66)\].

In one of the works, utilising structural MRI scans, Faturrahman, and others proposed a higher-level model by using DBNs (unsupervised learning) for predicting the progression of Alzheimer's disease (AD), with 90.59% sensitivity, 91.76% accuracy, and 92.96% specificity \[[67](#9789815305128-C2.html#r67)\]. While there is not a treatment for AD, early detection can aid in the implementation of plans to halt the deterioration and symptoms. Patel and others created a model for predicting depression diagnosis as well as treatment response by using the decision tree method and feature-rich type of data sets including cognitive behaviour scores, functional MRI, and age. For diagnostic and treatment responses, the model received scores of 87.27% and 89.47%, respectively \[[68](#9789815305128-C2.html#r68)\]. By using this predictive diagnosis, depression patients can be identified, and tailored treatment regimens can be created based on their individual reactions. Because of its clear benefits in classification, accuracy, specificity and sensitivity, in diagnosis and prediction, machine learning (ML) has a lot of scope for it in the medical profession. This is demonstrated by the current uses of ML in medical imaging.

### Genetic Engineering and Genomics

Genetic engineering has flourished since the innovation of the adaptable DNA system, which is known as Clustered Regularly Interspaced Short Palindromic Repeats (CRISPR) \[[69](#9789815305128-C2.html#r69)\]. This discovery of “programmable endonucleases” has greatly reduced the cost of genetic engineering, streamlined the process of genetic alteration, and aided in the process of diagnosis \[[70](#9789815305128-C2.html#r70)\]. Although the tool is not flawless, the current use of CRISPR to Cas (CRISPR-associated protein) editing, such as Cas-13a, and Cas-9 \[[71](#9789815305128-C2.html#r71)\] has altered genetic editing. Recently, a number of machine learning methods for anticipating off-target changes in Cas9 gene editing have been developed.

By utilising deep FFs (AUC score: 97%), and deep CNNs (AUC score: 97.2%) a new program created by Ka-Chun Wong and Jiecong Lin has enhanced the accuracy of predictions made by machine learning \[[72](#9789815305128-C2.html#r72)\]. Scientists are utilising Cas9 to create activity predictors and more dependable Cas9 variants in an effort to decrease error, given the potential for error and off-target alterations while utilising this technology. These models include RNA design tools guided by deep learning \[[73](#9789815305128-C2.html#r73)^(\-)[77](#9789815305128-C2.html#r77)\], hyper-accurate Cas9 variants \[[78](#9789815305128-C2.html#r78)\], and Cas9 variants with improved accuracy and fidelity \[[79](#9789815305128-C2.html#r79)^(\-)[81](#9789815305128-C2.html#r81)\].

In addition to CRISPR gene editing, O'Brien and associates have created a service that offers efficiency in nucleotide editing by utilising supervised learning and random forest algorithms to examine how various nucleotide compositions affect the effectiveness of homology-directed repair, or HDR \[[82](#9789815305128-C2.html#r82)\].

To determine the most effective way to precisely identify where to insert a certain point mutation and forecast HDR efficiency, they created the Computational Universal Nucleotide Editor (CUNE). Moreover, Pan and associates have created the ToxDL gene editing prediction model, which employs a CNN methodology to forecast protein toxicity *in vivo* based solely on sequence data \[[83](#9789815305128-C2.html#r83)\]. Pharacogenomics, a different area of genetic engineering, has also made great progress in using Artificial Intelligence and ML to establish stable dosages of increasingly popular drugs \[[84](#9789815305128-C2.html#r84)^(\-)[86](#9789815305128-C2.html#r86)\].

In one such trial, to lower the risk of acute rejection in patients receiving kidney transplants, Tang and others used an ML-based technique to find a stable Tacrolimus dose \[[87](#9789815305128-C2.html#r87)\]. Recently, oncology \[[88](#9789815305128-C2.html#r88)\], psychiatry \[[89](#9789815305128-C2.html#r89)\], neurology \[[90](#9789815305128-C2.html#r90)\], and bariatrics \[[91](#9789815305128-C2.html#r91)\], have all used machine learning in pharmacogenomics.

The fight against COVID-19 has also benefited greatly from genetic engineering applications of machine learning. Malone *et al.* conducted a recent study wherein they employed machine learning algorithms-based software to foretell which anti-genes possess the necessary characteristics of HLA-binding, processing, cell surface presentation, and T-cell recognition potential to be suitable for clinical aims for immunotherapy.

The group was able to effectively characterize both the “entire SARS-CoV2 proteome” and epitope hotspots by utilizing immunogenicity predictions from this technique in conjunction with antigen delivery to the infected host tissue. These findings provide guidelines for creating vaccinations against the virus that are universally applicable to all members of the world's population.

## CHALLENGES AND RISKS

Applications of machine learning in healthcare present innovative and progressive opportunities, but additionally, they raise unusual risks, challenges, and a healthy cynicism. We go over the primary risk factors here, including the possibility of prediction error and its effects, system security, and privacy vulnerabilities, despite the deficiency of information to generate consistent results. Among the difficulties are moral dilemmas, the removal of the human element from healthcare, as well as the interpretability and usefulness of the methods in a bedside environment.

The dependence of machine learning algorithms on probabilistic distributions and the likelihood of inaccuracy in diagnosis and prediction represent a significant danger.

A reasonable amount of uncertainty regarding the accuracy and validity of predictions given by ML-based approaches is also sparked by this. The consequences of using machine learning (ML) techniques that lead to a human’s death are dire, notwithstanding the fact that mistakes and probability likelihood are inherent in many facets of health care. A possible resolution would be to require stringent institutional and legal clearance from several organisations prior to implementing these machine learning-based techniques \[[92](#9789815305128-C2.html#r92), [93](#9789815305128-C2.html#r93)\]. Another strategy that can be used to prevent false-negative or false-positive diagnoses in extremely sensitive applications is human intervention and supervision by a qualified healthcare professional (*e.g.*, diagnosis of breast cancer or depression).

When current healthcare professionals are involved in the development and application of these strategies, adaptation rates may rise, and worries about declining career possibilities or a diminishing workforce may subside \[[94](#9789815305128-C2.html#r94)\].

A potential concern when utilising machine learning and deep learning techniques for the medical field is the lack of sufficient training and testing datasets with sufficiently big sample numbers to guarantee good prediction reproducibility and reliability. It is critical to emphasize the relevance of high-quality data because machine learning and deep learning algorithms 'learn' from the data. Furthermore, many learning networks and techniques require vast amounts of feature-rich data, which are not easily accessible and might potentially represent a limited sample of the population.

Furthermore, the data obtained from a variety of healthcare domains are diverse, lacking, and have a much higher number of features than samples. While creating and analysing the outcomes of techniques based on ML-based, these difficulties must be carefully considered. These obstacles might be addressed with the help of open science and the current movement for research data sharing. The risk to privacy and moral ramifications of using Healthcare applications of ML-based techniques should also be considered. Many machine learning (ML) techniques are developed and implemented using cloud-based platforms, with the understanding that these methods necessitate a significant amount of computing power as well as large-scale, readily scalable data storage.

Prior to developing any models, one of the first things that should be taken into consideration is enhanced data security and accountability, in light of privacy concerns and the delicate nature of medical data.

Regarding ethical considerations, scientists utilising machine learning techniques in the healthcare industry can easily draw insights from the genetic engineering domain, which has experienced a great deal of ethical discussion. There is an ongoing debate concerning the viability of using genetic engineering for genetic improvements which are long-lasting. Finding and correcting deleterious genetic abnormalities, like the HTT variant, which causes Huntington's disease, may be able to treat disorders like these with potentially fatal outcomes \[[95](#9789815305128-C2.html#r95)\].

On the other hand, developing therapies that modify a person's genome and the genome of their progeny while they are still prohibitively expensive could exacerbate the socioeconomic divide for those who cannot afford such care \[[96](#9789815305128-C2.html#r96)\]. Guidelines for the creation of AI machines have just recently surfaced. An Artificial Intelligence Model Governance Framework was developed by Singapore in 2019 to help private sector companies develop and apply AI in an ethical way \[[97](#9789815305128-C2.html#r97)\]. Additionally, an executive order was issued by the US government to “maintain American leadership in artificial intelligence” and to regulate the development of AI \[[98](#9789815305128-C2.html#r98)\]. These rules and principles, notwithstanding their severity, are in place to guarantee the conduct and advancement of ethical research.

Interpreting the results and determining if they are clinically applicable presents a significant challenge when applying machine learning to healthcare. Because machine learning (ML)-based approaches, especially deep learning-based approaches, have complex structures, it becomes very challenging to separate and identify the original features' contribution towards the prediction. The flexibility of ML-based techniques in healthcare has been severely hampered by a lack of transparency, despite the fact that it might not cause too much concern for other ML applications (like web searches). In the healthcare industry, the strategy for a solution is equally important as the real solution.

Increased adoption rates may also result from the participation of medical professionals and doctors in the creation, use, and testing of machine learning-based techniques. Furthermore, while there exists a reasonable degree of doubt regarding the possibility of a weakened interpersonal bond between a patient and primary care physician because of the growing adoption of machine learning-based techniques, these approaches offer a singular chance to enhance involvement. Research indicates that the notion of the physician-patient connection is gradually diminishing, with almost 25% of Americans lacking a primary care physician \[[99](#9789815305128-C2.html#r99)\]. Here, machine learning (ML) can offer special chances to boost patient participation in discussions about potential diagnoses and improve the effectiveness of Outreach Programmes.

When individuals speak with their PCPs, they could discover that early diagnosis provided by ML-based techniques also aids in the development of a healthy way of life. Lastly, according to a poll aimed at physicians, 5 percent of them spent fewer than nine minutes with their patients, and 56% of them spent 16 minutes or less \[[100](#9789815305128-C2.html#r100)\]. Reduced anxiety and more time spent one-on-one with patients are two benefits of using AI approaches for diagnosis and symptom monitoring. These improvements will enhance patient satisfaction and results.

## CONCLUSION

Even though the summary highlights the progress made in machine learning, much more work needs to be done in this area. The goal of many recent machine learning advancements in the medical field is to assist doctors and other healthcare professionals in providing patients with better care that is delivered more quickly, accurately, and with greater quality. To overcome the unavailability of data, the issues associated with constructing machine learning algorithms may be addressed by either designing and making improvements in data collection, dissemination, and storage or by algorithm development for handling unstructured data. Future developments may result in more accessible healthcare for lower-class countries and populations, as well as more economical medical exams and imaging systems. These developments could put an end to health inequities.

Improvements in the identification and dose of medications, the prediction of individual drug responses, and the application of genetic engineering to treat hereditary illnesses and mutations are all anticipated by scientists. ML has the potential to rethink patient care and strengthen the role of doctors. The present ML algorithms can serve as an excellent basis for ML's future advancements and use in the medical field, under the condition that the risks and challenges associated with its future use are acknowledged and addressed.

# Medical Imaging Using Machine Learning and Deep Learning: A Survey

  
  
**Uma Sharma^([1](#9789815305128-C3.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815305128-C3.html#cor1))****,** **Deeksha Sharma^([1](#9789815305128-C3.html#aff1))****,** **Pooja Pathak^([2](#9789815305128-C3.html#aff2))****,** **Sanjay Kumar Singh^([2](#9789815305128-C3.html#aff2))****,** **Pushpanjali Singh^([3](#9789815305128-C3.html#aff3))**  
¹ Inderprastha Engineering College, Ghaziabad, Uttar Pradesh 201009, India  
² ABES Engineering College, Ghaziabad, Uttar Pradesh 201009, India  
³ Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam University, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh 226031, India  
  

## Abstract

Machine learning and deep learning which are the subsets of Artificial intelligence, have numerous uses in medical imaging. Advancements in machine learning and deep learning led to drastic improvements in medical imaging fields like the evaluation of risks, recognition, identification, prediction, and treatment results. The decision-making power of computers based on artificial intelligence has elevated the effectiveness and efficiency of human decisions. Techniques based on machine learning and deep learning are not only effective and efficient but also speedy. In the medical field, the stage of the diagnosed disease is of great importance as the treatment and recovery rates depend on it. So based on the best and fastest decisions given by machine learning and deep learning techniques, medical practitioners can give their services in a better way.

We have given a summary of the methods used in medical imaging based on machine learning and deep learning algorithms with the benefits and pitfalls of these algorithms. These algorithms offer remarkable methods for classification, segmentation, and autonomous decision-making ability for the analysis of medical images.

**Keywords:** CNN, Deep learning, Medical image, Machine learning, RNN.  
  

* * *

^(\*) **Corresponding author Uma Sharma:** Inderprastha Engineering College, Ghaziabad, Uttar Pradesh 201009, India; E-mail: [umasharma107@gmail.com](mailto:umasharma107@gmail.com)  
  

## INTRODUCTION

In the field of medical imaging, significant impressions of the images give the idea about the overall health of an individual. But the main work is the study of these patterns. These impressions must be studied by a team of experts as only one view is not able to give the exact information. For best results, images are to

be analyzed repeatedly but still, this can give wrong results. Medical imaging has seen significant advancements with the application of machine learning (ML) and deep learning (DL) techniques. These technologies have shown promise in various aspects of medical imaging, including boosting efficiency, helping with treatment planning, and increasing diagnostic accuracy. These techniques can best extract the image features for making the diagnosis of interest \[[1](#9789815305128-C3.html#r1)\]. Many machine learning and deep learning techniques are available but artificial neural networks are the foundation of medical imaging \[[2](#9789815305128-C3.html#r2)^(\-)[4](#9789815305128-C3.html#r4)\]. Mathematical calculations are involved in artificial neural networks which imitate the function of the human brain.

The number of nodes decides the complexity and intelligence level of the decision or work done. CNN and RNN are the branches of neural networks also used in medical imaging. In CNN, after the starting layer, specific filter layers, activation layers, and pooling layers are used \[[5](#9789815305128-C3.html#r5)\]. In the end, minute features are recognized by using different layers. In the RNN (Recurrent Neural Network), due to recurrent linkages of the nodes, previous flow patterns are stored or remembered by the network. In medical imaging, the release of information is scattered over multiple layers with the relation among different layers. So it is the beauty of RNN that the related information can be extracted from the previous layers to better understand the subsequent layers \[[6](#9789815305128-C3.html#r6)\].

In the first part of the paper, we have discussed different medical imaging methods, then in the second part, machine learning techniques related to medical diagnosis are explained under the categories of supervised and unsupervised learning. In the next part, the role of deep learning in medical imaging is discussed. After that, open-source tools are given for the implementation of machine learning techniques. Finally, the conclusion is set out.

## MEDICAL IMAGE ANALYSIS

### Medical Imaging

Maximum techniques involved in medical imaging are based on small incisions so that doctors can see inside the body without surgery. In the medical field, numerous imaging methods are there, which are helpful in the identification and cure of many medical disorders. Each method has its advantages and disadvantages (Fig. **[1](#9789815305128-C3.html#f1)**).

![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
General medical imaging method. 

#### X-Ray Imaging

X-rays are ionizing radiations that interact with electrons in orbits rather than the nuclei as their energy levels are low. It is called ionizing energy as it provides energy that is sufficient for the ionization of atoms. Its wavelength is from 0.01nm to 10 nm. Medical diagnosis through X-ray is a noninvasive technique as X-rays can pass through the body without any incision. The type of material is responsible for the amount of X-ray absorption and scattering inside the matter.

In the X-ray tube, the filament wire emits electrons, and these electrons are drawn toward the revolving anode, which produces the alternating current. The focus point on the anode generates X-rays. Based on a particular area or problem, the contrast of rays can be increased for better picture contrast. Photostimulable phosphor (PSP) plates are used as receptors in X-ray radiography \[[7](#9789815305128-C3.html#r7)\]. After acquiring the digital image from PSP, the image is scaled and recorded. Fig. ([**2**](#9789815305128-C3.html#f2)) shows the concept of an X-ray imaging system.

![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
X-ray imaging system. 

#### Ultrasound Imaging

Ultrasound methods of medical imaging are quite popular these days. These methods are affordable as compared to other medical imaging techniques. Ultrasound methods are safe for the health of humans and animals as compared to magnetic resonance imaging and positron emission tomography \[[8](#9789815305128-C3.html#r8)\].

One disadvantage of ultrasound imaging is that it is affected by noise and has poor image quality as compared to other imaging techniques. Also, the expert operator is the main requirement of ultrasound imaging as the quality of the image depends upon the skill of the operator. Three-dimensional ultrasound images give better view and diagnosis as compared to two-dimensional images but 3D imaging is costlier than 2D imaging. So, research is going on converting 2D images into 3D images \[[9](#9789815305128-C3.html#r9)\]. In ultrasound imaging, machine learning is used according to the organ or body location classification for breast, liver, spine, and eye images, segmentation is used for the heart, brain, and placenta, and regression is used for the fetal brain, prostate, *etc*.

Diagnostic systems should be smart enough to make decisions based on ultrasound imaging. As shown in Fig. ([**3**](#9789815305128-C3.html#f3)), firstly the system should be trained for decision making and then the doctors can advise for immediate guidance \[[10](#9789815305128-C3.html#r10)\].

![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Machine learning-based us system. 

#### Magnetic Resonance Imaging

Machine Learning and deep learning will play a significant role in the study of magnetic resonance imaging in the upcoming years. With effective decision-making power, this system will provide better picture quality and reduced procuring time. It will also increase the speed of the work \[[11](#9789815305128-C3.html#r11)^(\-)[13](#9789815305128-C3.html#r13)\]. Increased speed is the solution to the problem of a limited number of scanners. Fig. ([**4**](#9789815305128-C3.html#f4)) shows the basic components of a magnetic resonance imaging system. In magnetic resonance imaging, a variety of tasks can be done using machine learning techniques; for example image classification, segmentation, and regression. In classification and segmentation, local labels are given to the images. After sensing, the image formation is the reconstruction in MRI, which is done with the help of regression that assigns continuous predictions for every pixel value in the picture \[[14](#9789815305128-C3.html#r14)\]. Image noise can also be reduced in MR imaging using machine learning. If the components of the forward model are not known, then the approximated function can be used.

#### Computerized Tomography

Computerized tomography (CT), commonly referred to as computed tomography or CT scan is a medical imaging procedure that produces comprehensive cross-sectional pictures of the body using X-ray technology. An X-ray machine with unique capabilities is used in the CT scan procedure, and it revolves around the patient. A computer processes the obtained data to produce intricate, two-dimensional pictures or three-dimensional reconstructions of the interior components.

![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
Magnetic resonance imaging components. 

#### Mammography

A specialist medical imaging method created especially for examining the breasts is called mammography. It is essential for the early identification and detection of breast cancer. Low-dose X-rays are used in mammograms to get finely detailed pictures of the breast tissue.

## MACHINE LEARNING

Healthcare is of utmost importance among all industries where security and authenticity are the highest priority. With the widespread adoption of image acquisition and medical data, many countries are taking measures to safeguard electronic patient records. This is particularly crucial as medical images play a pivotal role in telesurgery and telediagnosis, facilitating physician training and enabling discussions among healthcare professionals based on past diseases and treatments. The integration of Artificial Intelligence (AI) and Machine Learning (ML) becomes essential in this context, contributing to the advancement of medical practices and enhancing the effectiveness of healthcare systems \[[15](#9789815305128-C3.html#r15)\]. Machine Learning (ML) empowers doctors to make real-time decisions by leveraging extensive patient information and collective knowledge gained from experiences, moving beyond conventional programmed rules. Unlike traditional models based on predetermined equations, ML learns from inputs and outputs, adapting through training with the aid of optimization algorithms. This iterative process refines the models to provide accurate predictions for the given training data. The overarching goal of ML is to cultivate mathematical models capable of delivering beneficial outcomes when presented with new input data. ML finds diverse applications in the real world, including image processing, disease detection, natural language processing, speech recognition, and various other domains.

The healthcare system is experiencing a substantial surge in the demand for medical image services, encompassing various modalities such as radiography, endoscopy, mammography images (MG), nuclear medicine imaging, ultrasound images, magnetic resonance angiography (MRA), computed tomography (CT), magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), Positron Emission Tomography (PET), and pathological tests. Analyzing medical images poses a persistent challenge, compounded by the time-consuming nature of the process, often due to a shortage of radiologists. Machine Learning (ML) offers a promising solution by addressing these challenges, as it can learn and make predictions from data without requiring explicit programming, potentially enhancing the efficiency and accessibility of medical image analysis.

ML has three learning approaches, which are supervised learning, and unsupervised learning. Other learning approaches are reinforcement learning and recommender systems. The typical architecture of machine learning and deep learning is shown in Figs. ([**5**](#9789815305128-C3.html#f5) and [**6**](#9789815305128-C3.html#f6)). Supervision and training are the general concepts applied to both machine learning and deep learning.

![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F5.jpg)**Fig. (5))**  
Steps involved in machine learning with handcrafted medical images. ![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Workflow of Deep learning. 

### Machine Learning Techniques

#### Supervised Learning

Most of today's ML belongs to the supervised learning algorithm for medical applications. In this learning, models are based on known input and output data to predict future output. Supervised learning problems fall into two main categories: regression and classification. In regression, the goal is to predict outcomes with a continuous output or map input variables to a continuous function. In contrast, classification problems involve predicting outcomes with a discrete output, aiming to map input variables into distinct categories. For example, when dealing with a patient having a tumor, the objective is to predict whether the tumor is malignant or benign. This scenario exemplifies a classification-supervised learning task, where the focus is on categorizing input data into specific classes.

Numerous supervised ML algorithms are available in the literature, and each one has a unique method for predicting output values \[[16](#9789815305128-C3.html#r16)\]. There are no ML algorithms that meet all the requirements of applications in the field of medicine. Therefore, finding the right ML algorithm is just based on experiments. There are few supervised machine learning algorithms such as support vector machine (SVM), decision trees, neural networks, nearest neighbor, naive Bayes, and linear regression, which are mostly used for the security and classification of medical images. In clinical terms, the input data is in the form of medical images or data and the output is in the form of prediction of disease, diagnosis of disease, and tells about the present condition of the patient.

In Support Vector Machines (SVM), data is classified by determining a hyperplane, which serves as the decision boundary, effectively separating input data from other classes with the maximum margin of separation. The linear function divides the input data, and a loss function is employed to identify instances where the data falls on the incorrect side of the hyperplane if a nonlinear function is used \[[8](#9789815305128-C3.html#r8), [17](#9789815305128-C3.html#r17)\]. SVM boasts several advantages, including its flexibility in hyperplane selection, strong capability, and a reduction in computational cost, which help mitigate overfitting issues. However, a significant drawback of SVM is its high computational time requirement for training datasets \[[18](#9789815305128-C3.html#r18)\].

#### Unsupervised Learning

In unsupervised learning, we leave the computer to find labels, patterns, or structures in the given datasets. In this type of learning, we do not tell the algorithm in advance, we have only a bunch of data. A good illustration of unsupervised learning is clustering where the data lives in two different clusters. Clustering algorithms are used for a bunch of applications like organizing large computer clusters, social network analysis, market segmentation, object recognition, stock market data analysis, and also used for surprisingly astronomical data analysis \[[19](#9789815305128-C3.html#r19)\].

A major obstacle in the field of medical imaging is the interpretation of images for the diagnosis and treatment of complicated diseases from high-dimensional and diverse data. Both types of algorithms, supervised and unsupervised deep learning have produced encouraging achievements in the field of medical image analysis in recent years. There are several published studies on supervised deep learning, but there are very few thorough evaluations on unsupervised deep learning for medical image analysis. So large scope of research is present in the field of unsupervised learning for medical image analysis. Different types of auto encoders, Deep Belief Networks, Deep Boltzmann Machines, and Generative Adversarial Networks are a few of the models that come under the umbrella of unsupervised learning for medical image analysis. Deep Belief Networks are feedforward neural networks with numerous hidden layers. DBNs are simple, unsupervised networks, such as restricted Boltzmann machines (RBMs) or autoencoders, where the visible layer of each sub-network is derived from its hidden layer.

The restricted Boltzmann machine (RBM) is a type of generative stochastic artificial neural network capable of learning a probability distribution from its input data. RBMs find application in deep learning networks, where they contribute to the construction of deep belief networks. The process involves “stacking” RBMs and fine-tuning the resulting deep network is achieved through the use of gradient descent and backpropagation. This approach enables the creation of sophisticated and effective deep belief networks by leveraging the capabilities of RBMs.

There is also one more form of learning, semi-supervised learning that uses both supervised and unsupervised in which there is labeling of some parts of training data.

## DEEP LEARNING

The application of the Deep Learning Approach (DLA) in medical image analysis is emerging as a rapidly expanding field of research. Deep learning, a specialized form of machine learning, involves the direct learning of application tasks from input data and has proven highly effective in the context of medical imaging. The design of deep learning often utilizes Neural Network (NN) algorithms. The term “deep” refers to the incorporation of numerous network levels. While traditional NNs typically consist of two or three layers, deep learning networks can encompass hundreds of layers. This approach finds diverse applications in the medical field, including medical data security, disease prediction, pattern recognition, and various other areas.

The primary difference between Deep Learning (DL) and Machine Learning (ML) is that both approaches concentrate on feature learning, more precisely, the automatic extraction of data representations. Supervised machine learning techniques include Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN) and Recurrent Neural Networks (RNN). Unsupervised machine learning (ML) methods including Deep Belief Networks (DBNs), Boltzmann Machines, Autoencoders, and Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) are often used in the field of medical picture analysis. CNNs are frequently used because of their efficacy for medical image processing tasks such as segmentation, classification, object recognition, and registration.

### CNN (Convolution Neural Network)

The most widely utilized deep learning technique for medical applications is CNN, which is crucial for comprehending meaningful visual and structured data representations. Historically, before CNNs could be utilized efficiently, these qualities had to be manually designed or generated by less competent machine-learning models \[[20](#9789815305128-C3.html#r20)\]. Many of the manually created picture characteristics were typically abandoned once it was possible to use feature detectors discovered by CNNs because they turned out to be nearly useless when compared to feature detectors learned directly from the data.

#### Basic Building Blocks of CNN

With extremely few connections between the layers, a CNN is a specific type of artificial neural network designed to preserve spatial correlations in the data. The input to a CNN is set up in a grid layout, and it is then passed through layers that maintain these connections, with each layer action using a little portion of the preceding layer. According to Fig. ([**7**](#9789815305128-C3.html#f7)), a CNN uses Convolutional Layers, Rectified Linear Unit (RELU) Layers, and Pooling Layers to alter an input image made up of raw pixels. This feeds into a final Fully Connected Layer, which provides class scores or probabilities, classifying the input into the class with the greatest likelihood. So let us start with a brief description of the convolution layer.

![](Images/9789815305128-C3-F7.jpg)**Fig. (7))**  
Building blocks of CNN \[[25](#9789815305128-C3.html#r25)\]. 

#### Convolutional Layer

Operating on two functions is known as a convolutional layer, where one function is of input values or image pixel values and the other function is filter or kernel. Both functions are presented as an array of two numbers and the multiplication of two functions gives an output. This process is continued till the whole image process is reached and that gives a feature named an activation map. Basically in an activation map or feature map, the kernel is highly active to see the features like dots or straight or curved lines in an image. Sparse connections, weights sharing, and equivariant representation are three key ML ideas that are used in convolution \[[21](#9789815305128-C3.html#r21)\]. CNN has sparse connection means only a few input layers are connected to the next layer in contrast, a neural network includes all inputs, and each output neuron is coupled to a neuron. That gives important features to learn, which reduces the number of weights to be calculated and also greatly increases the efficiency of the algorithm.

#### Rectified Linear Unit (RELU) or Activation Layer

RELU is an activation function that only accepts positive values and lowers negative input values to zero. The RELU layer is a major aid in preventing the vanishing gradient problem by streamlining and optimizing training and computations. Sigmoid, tanh, leaky RELU, randomized RELU, and parametric RELUs are examples of frequent activation functions.

#### Pooling Layer

The pooling layer is positioned between the convolution layer and the RELU layer, serving to reduce the number of parameters to be computed as well as the height and width of the image. Various types of pooling exist, including max pooling, average pooling, and L2 normalization pooling, with max pooling being the most commonly employed. The primary role of max pooling is to select the highest input value within the kernel, discarding the remaining values. Essentially, it identifies the most dominant invariant feature within the pooling region.

#### Fully Connected Layer

In this, where every neuron in the previous layer is attached to every neuron in the last fully connected layer. Depending on the desired level of feature abstraction, there may be one or more fully connected layers in addition to convolution, pooling, and RELU. Essentially, this layer considers the set of powerful active features that would suggest the class to which the image belongs.

After Alex Net’s outstanding performance in 2012, CNNs have been employed more and more to improve the effectiveness of human physicians in processing medical images \[[22](#9789815305128-C3.html#r22)^(\-)[24](#9789815305128-C3.html#r24)\].

### RNN (Recurrent Neural Network)

It is used to process sequential data or information and used for content analysis tasks such as machine translation, computer speech recognition, language modeling used in NLP (natural language processing), and text prediction \[[26](#9789815305128-C3.html#r26), [27](#9789815305128-C3.html#r27)\] because of their capability to produce text.

In a basic Recurrent Neural Network (RNN), the output of each layer is combined with the input of the next layer, providing a form of contextual “memory.” Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) networks and Gated Recurrent Units (GRUs) have evolved from RNNs to address challenges related to the vanishing gradient problem during backpropagation through time. These are adjustments made to RNNs that allow them to hold long-term dependencies and ignore some of the collected information. For medical-related issues, RRN is used for image division.

Drawbacks of using deep learning for a clinical domain, and its prospects: Deep neural networks prove highly beneficial when making precise judgments based on intricate datasets. However, despite this significant advantage, employing deep learning methods comes with certain drawbacks. One primary limitation is the substantial requirement for both extensive training data and computational power. The process of training the model and obtaining accurate results is time-consuming due to these demands. A widely utilized deep learning technique is CNN, which necessitates labeled input for supervised learning. Manual labeling, particularly in case of medical images, poses a significant challenge. Addressing this challenge entails advancements in computational power, increased data storage, and continual enhancements in deep learning architectures. AI applications, specifically deep learning for medical image analysis, face the issue of unknown internal representations despite having well-defined inputs and outputs. Inherent problems such as noise and brightness issues in medical images are also prevalent. However, through preprocessing steps, the noise problem can be mitigated, contributing to ongoing improvements in performance levels over time \[[28](#9789815305128-C3.html#r28)\].

## MEDICAL IMAGING ANALYSIS WITH MACHINE LEARNING AND DEEP LEARNING

Medical image analysis's main goal is to locate the anatomically impacted areas of a disease, which helps doctors comprehend how lesions grow. Usually, this examination requires the following four crucial steps:

### Image Preprocessing

The preprocessing stage eliminates undesirable distortions from images to improve the information inside the images.

### Segmentation

This is the technique of separating particular areas, such as tumors or organs, for a more thorough examination of the image. It is essential for defining the region of interest (ROI) in the medical image data.

### Feature Extraction

Feature extraction is the process of extracting specific information from ROIs. The process of feature extraction entails gathering pertinent data that aids in the identification and description of these ROIs.

### Pattern Recognition or Classification

The last stage is classification, which entails grouping the ROIs based on the characteristics that were extracted. This stage facilitates the diagnostic procedure by helping to differentiate between various anatomical structure types or to spot anomalies.

The process begins with preprocessing to improve image quality, followed by segmentation to isolate regions of interest. Feature extraction focuses on capturing pertinent details from these regions, and classification utilizes the extracted features to categorize and identify the anatomical structures or abnormalities within the medical images. This systematic approach is crucial for providing valuable insights to healthcare professionals for accurate diagnosis and treatment planning \[[29](#9789815305128-C3.html#r29)\].

Many image analysis techniques are there. Out of these, pixel-based analysis is commonly used. In pixel-based analysis, direct image pixels are taken into account instead of the extracted part of the image \[[30](#9789815305128-C3.html#r30)\]. As several pixels in an image are very large, the time required to train the model is also very large in the case of machine learning with pixel-based analysis. Before applying this technique, the contrast of the image is enhanced using different filtering methods. For contrast enhancement, modification of histograms can also be made. Local binary patterns are used to apply machine learning to study medical images. It is used to give the spatial distribution of the edges of the image. A linear SVM classifier has been applied for good accuracy among all spatial edge descriptors \[[31](#9789815305128-C3.html#r31), [32](#9789815305128-C3.html#r32)\].

Neural networks are used in medical imaging to find the details of the disease. To classify cancerous cells and normal cells, neural networks are used. Neural network outcome for the classification of cells has great accuracy as compared to the previous voting methods of diagnosis \[[33](#9789815305128-C3.html#r33)\]. Machine learning techniques may be used to develop a set of rules of clinical characters that specifically characterize the situation for the set of clinical problems to have intelligent systems. Information can therefore be expressed as a set of straightforward rules or, more frequently, as a decision tree. The channelized SVM is applied after CHO *i.e*. channelized hoteling observer, to improve the quality of an image.

Convolutional neural networks, CNN, which is part of deep learning, are popularly used for image analysis. In medical imaging, classification is an important part that tells about the clinical results of interest. CNN can be applied to the large variety and large volume of three-dimensional data. In the analysis of cervical cytopathology images, CNN is applied for image segmentation and image classification \[[34](#9789815305128-C3.html#r34)\].

The registration process is used in medical pictures and data collected from various sensors. It is a procedure in which some basic parameters are identified as standards. Using deep learning techniques, a registration procedure is applied to find the degree of similarities between different images. Specifically, it is used in the field of medicine.

## OPEN-SOURCE TOOLS

To support several stages of the machine learning workflow, including data pre-processing, model development, evaluation, and deployment, various open-source machine learning tools are available. To implement healthcare applications, there are some popular machine learning tools like TensorFlow, Keras, Octave, PyTorch, OpenCV, sci-kit-learn, OpenAI Gym, SimpleITK, and many more. For medical image analysis, libraries like TensorFlow, PyTorch, scikit-learn, pandas, and NumPy are commonly used \[[35](#9789815305128-C3.html#r35)\].

TensorFlow is an open-source machine learning framework developed by Google. It is commonly used in medical imaging applications due to its flexibility, scalability, and extensive toolset for building and deploying machine learning models. Machine learning for medical imaging involves processes like data pre-processing, including image loading, resizing, normalization, and augmentation. The tools required for these processes are also available in Tensorflow. Pre-processing techniques are mainly provided by Tensorflow to prepare this data for model training. The training of different machine learning models like Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) used in medical imaging tasks can also be done by Tensorflow. CNN architectures are helpful to perform tasks like image classification, segmentation, object detection, and more. Through transfer learning, models trained on generic data can have their knowledge leveraged and customised to smaller datasets for particular medical imaging applications. TensorFlow can support medical image formats (DICOM, NIfTI, *etc*.) and carry out specialised tasks like image registration, analysis, or segmentation by integrating with medical imaging libraries such as SimpleITK, PyDICOM, or other domain-specific tools.

Keras is a high-level API for neural network construction that runs on top of TensorFlow and other frameworks. Deep neural networks can be built, trained, and deployed using Keras as it is a user-friendly interface. Keras can be used for a variety of tasks while dealing with medical imaging, including object detection, picture creation, segmentation, and image classification. It can also be used to obtain a dataset of medical images like X-rays, CT scans, and MRIs by providing annotations with required labels. The pre-processing of data can also be done on Keras by resizing, normalizing, augmenting, or carrying out additional required changes. Along with image classification architectures like Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) such as VGG, ResNet, or custom architectures, Keras also utilised architectures for segmentation like U-Net. When working with medical imaging, Keras can employ various tasks like building, training, evaluating, and predicting the model \[[36](#9789815305128-C3.html#r36)\].

There are some popular machine-learning libraries used in medical imaging. PyTorch is one of them as it is a popular open-source machine learning library, and has been extensively used in various healthcare applications due to its flexibility, efficiency, and robustness in developing deep learning models. PyTorch is developed by Facebook's AI Research lab. PyTorch has been used for tasks like reconstruction, classification, and segmentation of medical images. To diagnose diseases, segment tumours, and locate organs in medical pictures like MRIs, CT scans, and X-rays, deep learning models developed with PyTorch have been applied. Predictive models for diseases like cancer, diabetes, and cardiovascular diseases can be developed using PyTorch. The outcomes of these models are determined with patient data such as electronic health records (EHRs), lab reports, medical images, progression, or patients’ physical health. One other application of PyTorch is developing models for wearable devices like smartwatches and fitness trackers \[[37](#9789815305128-C3.html#r37), [38](#9789815305128-C3.html#r38)\].

## CONCLUSION

The existing pool of medical information proves inadequate for making clinical decisions. This paper provides a concise introduction to medical images and explores the utilization of machine learning and deep learning in their analysis. Fundamentals of both supervised and unsupervised algorithms are covered. The survey reveals that CNN algorithms, having demonstrated favorable outcomes, are prevalently employed in medical image analysis. Nevertheless, there persist several challenges in medical image processing that necessitate attention. Deep learning algorithms emerge as potent tools, particularly beneficial for the upcoming generation of radiologists with limited experience. Their efficiency is also noteworthy in accurately predicting diseases within shorter time frames.
